FR-RPT1 requires every expanded EvalSpec to be \"\n            \"present in evals[] (SKIPPED rows stay in the list).\"\n        )\n\n\ndef _check_invariant(summary: RunSummary) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Enforce FR-RPT1 ``len(evals) == counts.expanded_n_prime``.\n\n    Raised *before* any file is written so the writer never leaves a\n    partial summary on disk that contradicts the manifest accounting.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    expected = summary.counts.expanded_n_prime\n    actual = len(summary.evals)\n    if expected != actual:\n        raise ReporterContractViolation(\n            expected=expected, actual=actual, run_id=summary.run_id\n        )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Markdown renderer\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _format_duration(seconds: float) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render a duration in a human-readable form (Xm Ys or Y.Ys).\n\n    Stable across Python versions; sub-second values keep one decimal so\n    the markdown table does not flatten 0.05s vs 0.5s into \"0s\".\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if seconds >= 60:\n        minutes, rem = divmod(seconds, 60)\n        return f\"{int(minutes)}m {rem:.1f}s\"\n    return f\"{seconds:.1f}s\"\n\n\ndef _row_note(outcome: EvalOutcome) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Pick a short note for the per-eval markdown row.\"\"\"\n\n    if outcome.status == \"SKIPPED\":\n        if outcome.skip_reason:\n            return outcome.skip_reason\n        return \"—\"\n    if outcome.status == \"ERRORED\" and outcome.error_class:\n        return outcome.error_class\n    return \"—\"\n\n\ndef render_summary_markdown(summary: RunSummary) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the ``summary.md`` body as a single string.\n\n    Output shape follows design-spec §9 (human-readable summary):\n\n    * Header line: ``# Eval Run: <started_at> / <run_id>``.\n    * Meta line:   ``**Suite:** ... | **Parallel:** N | **Duration:** ...``\n    * Result line: ``## Result: passed/failed/skipped tally``.\n    * Per-eval table with ``ID | Title | Status | Duration | Notes``.\n    * Failures block: per-failure detail block with title, expects, and\n      artifacts when present.\n    * Counts block: rendered last so reviewers can sanity-check the\n      N'-vs-K accounting against the table above.\n\n    Trailing newline so concatenation with downstream content stays\n    clean. Byte-stable for a given ``RunSummary`` (the input dataclass\n    is frozen; iteration follows the tuple ordering the orchestrator\n    constructed).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    _check_invariant(summary)\n\n    totals = summary.totals\n    counts = summary.counts\n\n    lines: list[str] = []\n    lines.append(f\"# Eval Run: {summary.started_at} / {summary.run_id}\")\n    lines.append(\n        f\"**Suite:** {summary.suite} | **Parallel:** {summary.parallel} | \"\n        f\"**Duration:** {_format_duration(summary.duration_sec)}\"\n    )\n    lines.append(\"\")\n    lines.append(\n        \"## Result: \"\n        f\"{totals.passed} passed, {totals.failed} failed, \"\n        f\"{totals.skipped} skipped, {totals.errored} errored, \"\n        f\"{totals.interrupted} interrupted, {totals.timeout} timeout\"\n    )\n    lines.append(\"\")\n\n    lines.append(\"| ID | Title | Status | Duration | Notes |\")\n    lines.append(\"|---|---|---|---|---|\")\n    for outcome in summary.evals:\n        lines.append(\n            f\"| {outcome.eval_id} | {outcome.title} | {outcome.status} | \"\n            f\"{_format_duration(outcome.duration_sec)} | {_row_note(outcome)} |\"\n        )\n\n    failures = [o for o in summary.evals if o.status in _FAILURE_STATUSES]\n    if failures:\n        lines.append(\"\")\n        lines.append(f\"## Failures ({len(failures)})\")\n        for outcome in failures:\n            lines.append(\"\")\n            lines.append(f\"### {outcome.eval_id}: {outcome.title}\")\n            lines.append(f\"**Status:** {outcome.status}\")\n            if outcome.error_class:\n                lines.append(f\"**Error class:** {outcome.error_class}\")\n            if outcome.expects:\n                for expect in outcome.expects:\n                    bullet = \"✅\" if expect.passed else \"❌\"\n                    msg = expect.message or \"\"\n                    lines.append(f\"- {bullet} `{expect.name}` — {msg}\".rstrip())\n            if outcome.artifacts:\n                lines.append(\"**Artifacts:**\")\n                for name in sorted(outcome.artifacts):\n                    lines.append(f\"- `{name}` → `{outcome.artifacts[name]}`\")\n\n    lines.append(\"\")\n    lines.append(\"## Counts\")\n    lines.append(f\"- manifest_n: {counts.manifest_n}\")\n    lines.append(f\"- expanded_n_prime: {counts.expanded_n_prime}\")\n    lines.append(f\"- kept_k: {counts.kept_k}\")\n    lines.append(f\"- skipped_s: {counts.skipped_s}\")\n    lines.append(\n        \"- kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime: \"\n        f\"{str(counts.kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime).lower()}\"\n    )\n\n    if summary.finished_at:\n        lines.append(\"\")\n        lines.append(f\"_Finished at {summary.finished_at}._\")\n\n    lines.append(\"\")\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# JSON renderer\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef render_summary_json(summary: RunSummary) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the ``summary.json`` payload as a string.\n\n The 8-field\n215\t    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n216\t\n217\t    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n218\t      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n219\t      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n220\t    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n221\t      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n222\t      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n223\t    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n224\t      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n225\t    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n226\t      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n227\t      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n228\t    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. Same\n229\t      typing rationale as ``expected``.\n230\t    * ``message``     — human-readable one-liner summarising the diff;\n231\t      defaults to ``\"\"`` so a caller can omit it when the diff between\n232\t      ``expected`` / ``actual`` is self-explanatory.\n233\t    * ``artifact_ref`` — optional path-or-URI to a side-car artifact\n234\t      (rendered diff file, dumped JSONL, captured stderr). ``None`` when no\n235\t      artifact was written. The Reporter resolves the ref against the run's\n236\t      scratch root before emitting the JSON report.\n237\t    * ``traceback``   — optional Python traceback string captured when the\n238\t      Expect itself raised. ``None`` for assertion failures that produced a\n239\t      clean diff rather than an exception.\n240\t\n241\t    The dataclass is frozen so a Reporter consumer cannot mutate failure\n242\t    records mid-render. Equality is structural across all 8 fields, which\n243\t    keeps deduplication straightforward.\n244\t    \"\"\"\n245\t\n246\t    eval_id: str\n247\t    expect_id: str\n248\t    expect_name: str\n249\t    expected: Any\n250\t    actual: Any\n251\t    message: str = \"\"\n252\t    artifact_ref: Optional[str] = None\n253\t    traceback: Optional[str] = None\n254\t\n255\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n256\t        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-005.\n257\t\n258\t        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`` so\n259\t        the output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or\n260\t        dataclass internals. ``expected`` / ``actual`` are passed through\n261\t        as-is — callers populate them with JSON-shaped payloads, so the\n262\t        reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result directly.\n263\t        \"\"\"\n264\t\n265\t        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS}\n266\t\n267\t\n268\t# Field order mirrors DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable\n269\t# across Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n270\t_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n271\t    \"eval_id\",\n272\t    \"title\",\n273\t    \"status\",\n274\t    \"duration_sec\",\n275\t    \"expects\",\n276\t    \"skip_reason\",\n277\t    \"skip_flag_triggered\",\n278\t    \"artifacts\",\n279\t    \"error_class\",\n280\t)\n281\t\n282\t\n283\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n284\tclass EvalOutcome:\n285\t    \"\"\"Per-expanded-eval outcome record emitted by ``EvalRunner`` (DM-001).\n286\t\n287\t    Nine-field contract consumed by the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) and\n288\t    exit-code logic. The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n289\t    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n290\t\n291\t    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded\n292\t      by FR-SCH2 (T01.05) so the Reporter can use it verbatim in artifact\n293\t      filenames and summary keys.\n294\t    * ``title``               — human-readable eval title (from ``EvalSpec``);\n295\t      copied onto the outcome so the Reporter does not need to re-resolve the\n296\t      manifest at render time.\n297\t    * ``status``              — one of the 8 ``EvalStatus`` literals: ``PASS``,\n298\t      ``FAIL``, ``ERRORED``, ``TIMEOUT``, ``INTERRUPTED``, ``SKIPPED``,\n299\t      ``XFAIL``, ``XPASS``. Validated in ``__post_init__``: any other value\n300\t      raises ``ValueError``.\n301\t    * ``duration_sec``        — wall-clock seconds the eval ran (lifecycle\n302\t      start to teardown). ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n303\t    * ``expects``             — tuple of ``ExpectResult`` records, one per\n304\t      assertion the eval evaluated. Stored as a tuple so the frozen dataclass\n305\t      preserves equality and hashability; an empty tuple is the default for\n306\t      ``SKIPPED`` outcomes (no assertions executed).\n307\t    * ``skip_reason``         — free-form reason string when ``status`` is\n308\t      ``SKIPPED`` (e.g. ``\"capability 'mcp.tavily' unresolved\"``). ``None``\n309\t      otherwise. DM-001 does not couple ``status == SKIPPED`` to a non-None\n310\t      reason — empty-skip rows are documented elsewhere (loader paths) and\n311\t      the model accepts both shapes.\n312\t    * ``skip_flag_triggered`` — name of the flag/capability that triggered\n313\t      the skip (e.g. ``\"mcp.tavily\"``, ``\"--include-skipped\"``). ``None``\n314\t      when the skip was not flag-driven.\n315\t    * ``artifacts``           — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n316\t      run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable\n317\t      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n318\t      an empty mapping is the default.\n319\t    * ``error_class``         — fully-qualified Python exception class name\n320\t      when ``status`` is ``ERRORED`` (e.g. ``\"builtins.RuntimeError\"``).\n321\t      ``None`` for non-error outcomes. The Reporter uses this to group\n322\t      identical harness failures across runs.\n323\t\n324\t    Field declaration order matches DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` and\n325\t    ``dataclasses.asdict`` produce stable, reviewable JSON.\n326\t    \"\"\"\n327\t\n328\t    eval_id: str\n329\t    title: str\n330\t    status: EvalStatus\n331\t    duration_sec: float\n332\t    expects: tuple[ExpectResult, ...] = ()\n333\t    skip_reason: Optional[str] = None\n334\t    skip_flag_triggered: Optional[str] = None\n335\t    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n336\t    error_class: Optional[str] = None\n337\t\n338\t    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n339\t        # Validate status membership against the authoritative literal set.\n340\t        # The acceptance criteria require \"Invalid status raises ValueError;\n341\t        # valid statuses are exactly the 8 listed in DM-001\" — a runtime\n342\t        # check is the cheapest enforcement that survives mypy being absent\n343\t        # at runtime and callers that build outcomes from dynamic strings\n344\t        # (e.g. JSON replay, partial summary on SIGINT).\n345\t        if self.status not in EVAL_STATUSES:\n346\t            raise ValueError(\n347\t                f\"EvalOutcome.status must be one of {EVAL_STATUSES!r}; \"\n348\t                f\"got {self.status!r}\"\n349\t            )\n350\t\n351\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n352\t        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-001.\n353\t\n354\t        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS`` so the\n355\t        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n356\t        internals. Nested ``ExpectResult`` values are unwrapped via their\n357\t        own ``to_dict()`` so the Reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result\n358\t        directly without bespoke recursion. ``artifacts`` is shallow-copied\n359\t        into a plain ``dict`` so callers that mutate the returned mapping do\n360\t        not affect the immutable source.\n361\t        \"\"\"\n362\t\n363\t        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n364\t        for name in _EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS:\n365\t            value = getattr(self, name)\n366\t            if name == \"expects\":\n367\t                payload[name] = [item.to_dict() for item in value]\n368\t            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n369\t                payload[name] = dict(value)\n370\t            else:\n371\t                payload[name] = value\n372\t        return payload\n373\t\n374\t\n375\t# Field order mirrors DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n376\t# Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n377\t_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n378\t    \"eval_id\",\n379\t    \"outcome\",\n380\t    \"start\",\n381\t    \"end\",\n382\t    \"duration_sec\",\n383\t    \"stdout\",\n384\t    \"stderr\",\n385\t    \"artifacts\",\n386\t    \"error\",\n387\t)\n388\t\n389\t\n390\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n391\tclass EvalResult:\n392\t    \"\"\"Reporter-facing per-eval result record (DM-003).\n393\t\n394\t    Nine-field contract consumed by COMP-008 Reporter (T03.13) as its primary\n395\t    per-eval input. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n396\t    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n397\t    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n398\t    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n399\t    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n400\t\n401\t    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n402\t      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n403\t      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n404\t    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n405\t      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n406\t      cannot be swapped after construction.\n407\t    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n408\t      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n409\t      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n410\t      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n411\t    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n412\t      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n413\t    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n414\t      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n415\t      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n416\t      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n417\t      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n418\t      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n419\t    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n420\t      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n421\t      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n422\t    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n423\t      as ``stdout``.\n424\t    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n425\t      relative) path produced by the eval. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n452\t        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n453\t        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n454\t        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n455\t        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n456\t        if self.start and self.end:\n457\t            computed = (\n458\t                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n459\t            ).total_seconds()\n460\t            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; the\n461\t            # documented escape hatch is ``object.__setattr__``.\n462\t            object.__setattr__(self, \"duration_sec\", computed)\n463\t\n464\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n465\t        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-003.\n466\t\n467\t        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS`` so the\n468\t        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n469\t        internals. ``outcome`` is unwrapped via ``EvalOutcome.to_dict()`` so\n470\t        the Reporter never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-\n471\t        copied into a plain ``dict``. ``error`` is converted to a\n472\t        ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` mapping (or ``None``) so\n473\t        the payload remains JSON-serializable — bare ``BaseException``\n474\t        instances cannot pass through ``json.dumps``.\n475\t        \"\"\"\n476\t\n477\t        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n478\t        for name in _EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS:\n479\t            value = getattr(self, name)\n480\t            if name == \"outcome\":\n481\t                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n482\t            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n483\t                payload[name] = dict(value)\n484\t            elif name == \"error\":\n485\t                payload[name] = _render_error(value)\n486\t            else:\n487\t                payload[name] = value\n488\t        return payload\n489\t\n490\t\n491\tdef _render_error(error: Optional[BaseException]) -> Optional[dict[str, str]]:\n492\t    \"\"\"Render an exception as a JSON-safe mapping.\n493\t\n494\t    Returns ``None`` when no error was captured. Otherwise emits a two-key\n495\t    mapping with the fully-qualified class name and the ``str()`` rendering\n496\t    of the exception. We deliberately do *not* serialise the traceback here\n497\t    — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source;\n498\t    DM-003's ``error`` is the Reporter-facing summary handle.\n499\t    \"\"\"\n500\t\n501\t    if error is None:\n502\t        return None\n503\t    cls = type(error)\n504\t    qualified = f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n505\t    return {\"type\": qualified, \"message\": str(error)}\n506\t\n507\t\n508\t# Field order mirrors DM-010 verbatim so attribute traversal, factory\n509\t# construction, and any future ``to_dict()`` consumer iterate in the same\n510\t# canonical order the roadmap row enumerates.\n511\t_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n512\t    \"eval_spec\",\n513\t    \"home\",\n514\t    \"home_path\",\n515\t    \"artifacts_dir\",\n516\t    \"run_dir\",\n517\t    \"env\",\n518\t    \"stdout_path\",\n519\t    \"stderr_path\",\n520\t    \"transcript_path\",\n521\t    \"jsonl_paths\",\n522\t    \"exit_code\",\n523\t    \"stdout\",\n524\t    \"stderr\",\n525\t    \"duration_sec\",\n526\t    \"artifacts\",\n527\t)\n528\t\n529\t\n530\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n531\tclass EvalContext:\n532\t    \"\"\"Runtime context passed to every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-010).\n533\t\n534\t    Fifteen-field immutable view fed to each assertion primitive\n535\t    (FR-EXP1 / T04.01) after the runner (FR-LC1 / T03.04) has finished\n536\t    spawning the Claude subprocess and captured its outputs. Every\n537\t    primitive ``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` / ``Expect.exit_code``\n538\t    receives this exact record so manifest authors get a stable shape\n539\t    regardless of which runner internal path produced the eval.\n540\t\n541\t    Field declaration order matches DM-010 verbatim (see\n542\t    ``_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS``) so structural traversal, JSON dumping by\n543\t    downstream tools, and review diffs stay deterministic.\n544\t\n545\t    * ``eval_spec``       — parsed manifest row (DM-002 / T01.03). Stored\n546\t      by-reference; ``EvalSpec`` is itself frozen so the runner cannot\n547\t      mutate the spec after the context is built. ExpectCallables read\n548\t      ``eval_spec.id`` / ``eval_spec.expects`` / ``eval_spec.timeout_sec``\n549\t      directly off the context without having to re-thread the spec.\n550\t    * ``home``            — the per-eval ``HomeIsolation`` record\n551\t      (DM-006 / T02.04). Stored as a separate ``Path`` so\n559\t      ExpectCallables that only need the path avoid the\n560\t      ``home.home_path`` property indirection (and the ``RuntimeError``\n561\t      it raises when setup has not run — by the time the context is\n562\t      built the setup has already completed). The factory keeps\n563\t      ``home.home_path == home_path`` invariant.\n564\t    * ``artifacts_dir``   — directory the runner writes per-eval\n565\t      artifacts under (PTY transcripts, hook outputs, captured\n566\t      side-cars). Resolved beneath ``home_path``; primitives that emit\n567\t      diff side-cars (``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` failures) drop\n568\t      them here.\n569\t    * ``run_dir``         — the run-scoped scratch directory the\n570\t      orchestrator created (FR-G2 / T03.16). One ``run_dir`` per\n571\t      ``superclaude eval run`` invocation; per-eval ``HOME``s and\n572\t      artifact directories live beneath it.\n573\t    * ``env``              — frozen mapping of environment variables that\n574\t      were exported to the Claude subprocess (the union of\n575\t      ``HomeIsolation.env()`` plus ``IsolationLayers.env_vars`` plus any\n576\t      caller overrides). ExpectCallables that need raw bytes (``Expect.stdout``)\n582\t      open this path.\n583\t    * ``stderr_path``      — same for stderr.\n584\t    * ``transcript_path``  — path to the raw PTY transcript (pre-strip,\n585\t      ANSI escapes preserved). Used by tooling that needs the byte-for-\n586\t      byte capture; ExpectCallables generally use ``stdout_path`` /\n587\t      ``stderr_path`` instead.\n588\t    * ``jsonl_paths``      — frozen mapping of named JSONL log file\n589\t      paths (e.g. ``{\"hook_log\": ..., \"telemetry\": ...}``). Per-eval\n590\t      runtime logs land here; ``Expect.jsonl(name=\"hook_log\", ...)``\n591\t      resolves the path through this mapping. Stored as a\n592\t      ``MappingProxyType`` for the same immutability reason as ``env``.\n593\t    * ``exit_code``        — captured exit code of the Claude subprocess\n594\t      (``int``). The runner records the value the PTY driver observed\n595\t      after ``wait()``; ``Expect.exit_code`` reads it.\n596\t    * ``stdout``           — full captured stdout as a ``str``. Mirrors\n597\t      what ``stdout_path`` points at so ExpectCallables can read it\n598\t      in-memory without re-opening the file. Empty when no output was\n599\t      captured.\n600\t    * ``stderr``           — full captured stderr; same rationale as\n601\t      ``stdout``.\n602\t    * ``duration_sec``     — wall-clock seconds the eval body ran\n603\t      (subprocess spawn to exit). ``float`` for sub-millisecond\n604\t      resolution. ``Expect.duration`` reads this verbatim.\n605\t    * ``artifacts``        — frozen mapping of artifact-name to absolute\n606\t      (or run-relative) path produced by the eval. Mirrors\n607\t      ``EvalOutcome.artifacts`` (DM-001) — the post-run inventory the\n608\t      runner builds before emitting the outcome. Stored as a\n609\t      ``MappingProxyType`` so the context cannot be tampered with by\n610\t      ExpectCallables that share the record across primitives.\n611\t\n612\t    The dataclass is ``frozen=True`` so any attempted ``ctx.foo = bar``\n613\t    raises :class:`dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError` — the contract every\n614\t    ExpectCallable can rely on. Mapping fields are additionally wrapped\n615\t    in :class:`types.MappingProxyType` to prevent the more subtle attack\n616\t    of mutating ``ctx.env[\"HOME\"]`` (frozen-dataclass blocks\n617\t    ``__setattr__``, not method calls on field values).\n618\t\n619\t    Use :meth:`from_runner_state` to construct an ``EvalContext`` from\n620\t    the runner's internal state; direct ``EvalContext(...)`` construction\n621\t    is permitted (tests build minimal contexts for ExpectCallable unit\n622\t    tests) but the factory is the single place runner-internal mappings\n623\t    are wrapped in ``MappingProxyType`` and the ``home.home_path ==\n624\t    home_path`` invariant is enforced.\n625\t    \"\"\"\n626\t\n627\t    eval_spec: \"EvalSpec\"\n628\t    home: \"HomeIsolation\"\n629\t    home_path: Path\n630\t    artifacts_dir: Path\n631\t    run_dir: Path\n632\t    env: Mapping[str, str]\n633\t    stdout_path: Path\n634\t    stderr_path: Path\n635\t    transcript_path: Path\n636\t    jsonl_paths: Mapping[str, Path]\n637\t    exit_code: int\n638\t    stdout: str\n639\t    stderr: str\n640\t    duration_sec: float\n641\t    artifacts: Mapping[str, str]\n642\t\n643\t    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n644\t        # Wrap mapping fields in MappingProxyType so callers that share\n645\t        # the context across ExpectCallables cannot mutate the underlying\n646\t        # mapping. Resolves ``home_path`` off the ``HomeIsolation`` instance so\n679\t           the runner does not duplicate ``home.home_path`` at the call\n680\t           site. The property accessor raises\n681\t           :class:`RuntimeError` if :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` has not\n682\t           run — which is exactly the failure we want surfaced at\n683\t           context construction (rather than later when an\n684\t           ExpectCallable reads ``ctx.home_path``).\n685\t        2. Wraps the three mapping arguments (``env``, ``jsonl_paths``,\n686\t           ``artifacts``) in :class:`types.MappingProxyType` so the\n687\t           context's mapping fields are read-only views of the runner's\n688\t           internal state. ``__post_init__`` would also wrap them, but\n689\t           doing it here keeps the wrap close to the call site for\n690\t           readability.\n691\t        3. Produces a deterministic instance: every ``EvalContext``\n692\t           built from the same arguments compares equal (the\n693\t           ``@dataclass``-generated ``__eq__`` covers the 15 fields; the\n694\t           mapping proxies compare equal when their underlying dicts do).\n695\t\n696\t        Keyword-only arguments are required so future field additions\n697\t        (e.g. when DM-010 home=home,\n704\t            home_path=home.home_path,\n705\t            artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n706\t            run_dir=run_dir,\n707\t            env=MappingProxyType(dict(env)),\n708\t            stdout_path=stdout_path,\n709\t            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n710\t            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n711\t            jsonl_paths=MappingProxyType(dict(jsonl_paths)),\n712\t            exit_code=exit_code,\n713\t            stdout=stdout,\n714\t            stderr=stderr,\n715\t            duration_sec=duration_sec,\n716\t The 8-field\n    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n\n    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n\n    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded\n      by FR-SCH2 (T01.05) so the Reporter can use it verbatim in artifact\n      filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``title``               — human-readable eval title (from ``EvalSpec``);\n      copied onto the outcome so the Reporter does not need to re-resolve the\n      manifest at render time.\n    * ``status``              — one of the 8 ``EvalStatus`` literals: ``PASS``,\n      ``FAIL``, ``ERRORED``, ``TIMEOUT``, ``INTERRUPTED``, ``SKIPPED``,\n      ``XFAIL``, ``XPASS``. Validated in ``__post_init__``: any other value\n      raises ``ValueError``.\n    * ``duration_sec``        — wall-clock seconds the eval ran (lifecycle\n      start to teardown). ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``expects``             — tuple of ``ExpectResult`` records, one per\n      assertion the eval evaluated. Stored as a tuple so the frozen dataclass\n      preserves equality and hashability; an empty tuple is the default for\n      ``SKIPPED`` outcomes (no assertions executed).\n    * ``skip_reason``         — free-form reason string when ``status`` is\n      ``SKIPPED`` (e.g. ``\"capability 'mcp.tavily' unresolved\"``). ``None``\n      otherwise. DM-001 does not couple ``status == SKIPPED`` to a non-None\n      reason — empty-skip rows are documented elsewhere (loader paths) and\n      the model accepts both shapes.\n    * ``skip_flag_triggered`` — name of the flag/capability that triggered\n      the skip (e.g. ``\"mcp.tavily\"``, ``\"--include-skipped\"``). ``None``\n      when the skip was not flag-driven.\n    * ``artifacts``           — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n      run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable\n      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n      an empty mapping is the default.\n    * ``error_class``         — fully-qualified Python exception class name\n      when ``status`` is ``ERRORED`` (e.g. ``\"builtins.RuntimeError\"``).\n      ``None`` for non-error outcomes. The Reporter uses this to group\n      identical harness failures across runs.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` and\n    ``dataclasses.asdict`` produce stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    title: str\n    status: EvalStatus\n    duration_sec: float\n    expects: tuple[ExpectResult, ...] = ()\n    skip_reason: Optional[str] = None\n    skip_flag_triggered: Optional[str] = None\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error_class: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # Validate status membership against the authoritative literal set.\n        # The acceptance criteria require \"Invalid status raises ValueError;\n        # valid statuses are exactly the 8 listed in DM-001\" — a runtime\n        # check is the cheapest enforcement that survives mypy being absent\n        # at runtime and callers that build outcomes from dynamic strings\n        # (e.g. JSON replay, partial summary on SIGINT).\n        if self.status not in EVAL_STATUSES:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"EvalOutcome.status must be one of {EVAL_STATUSES!r}; \"\n                f\"got {self.status!r}\"\n            )\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-001.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. Nested ``ExpectResult`` values are unwrapped via their\n        own ``to_dict()`` so the Reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result\n        directly without bespoke recursion. ``artifacts`` is shallow-copied\n        into a plain ``dict`` so callers that mutate the returned mapping do\n        not affect the immutable source.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"expects\":\n                payload[name] = [item.to_dict() for item in value]\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n# Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"outcome\",\n    \"start\",\n    \"end\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"stdout\",\n    \"stderr\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalResult:\n    \"\"\"Reporter-facing per-eval result record (DM-003).\n\n    Nine-field contract consumed by COMP-008 Reporter (T03.13) as its primary\n    per-eval input. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n\n    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n      cannot be swapped after construction.\n    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n      as ``stdout``.\n    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n      relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping``\n      so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract; an empty\n      mapping is the default. ``to_dict()`` returns a shallow copy so callers\n      that mutate the returned mapping do not affect the immutable source.\n    * ``error``        — optional ``BaseException`` instance captured when the\n      *harness* (not an assertion) raised. ``None`` for clean runs. The\n      Reporter renders this as ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` in\n      ``to_dict()``; the live exception instance is kept on the dataclass so\n      diagnostic tools can inspect the traceback if it is still attached.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` produces\n    stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    outcome: EvalOutcome\n    start: str\n    end: str\n    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n    stdout: str = \"\"\n    stderr: str = \"\"\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # DM-003 acceptance: ``duration_sec`` is computed from ``end - start``\n        # consistently. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n        if self.start and self.end:\n            computed = (\n                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n            ).total_seconds()\n            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; the\n            # documented escape hatch is ``object.__setattr__``.\n            object.__setattr__(self, \"duration_sec\", computed)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-003.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. ``outcome`` is unwrapped via ``EvalOutcome.to_dict()`` so\n        the Reporter never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-\n        copied into a plain ``dict``. ``error`` is converted to a\n        ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` mapping (or ``None``) so\n        the payload remains JSON-serializable — bare ``BaseException``\n        instances cannot pass through ``json.dumps``.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"outcome\":\n                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            elif name == \"error\":\n                payload[name] = _render_error(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\ndef _render_error(error: Optional[BaseException]) -> Optional[dict[str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Render an exception as a JSON-safe mapping.\n\n    Returns ``None`` when no error was captured. Otherwise emits a two-key\n    mapping with the fully-qualified class name and the ``str()`` rendering\n    of the exception. We deliberately do *not* serialise the traceback here\n    — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source;\n    DM-003's ``error`` is the Reporter-facing summary handle.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if error is None:\n        return None\n    cls = type(error)\n    qualified = f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n    return {\"type\": qualified, \"message\": str(error)}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-010 verbatim so attribute traversal, factory\n# construction, and any future ``to_dict()`` consumer iterate in the same\n# canonical order the roadmap row enumerates.\n_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_spec\",\n    \"home\",\n    \"home_path\",\n    \"artifacts_dir\",\n    \"run_dir\",\n    \"env\",\n    \"stdout_path\",\n    \"stderr_path\",\n    \"transcript_path\",\n    \"jsonl_paths\",\n    \"exit_code\",\n    \"stdout\",\n    \"stderr\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalContext:\n    \"\"\"Runtime context passed to every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-010).\n\n    Fifteen-field immutable view fed to each assertion primitive\n    (FR-EXP1 / T04.01) after the runner (FR-LC1 / T03.04) has finished\n    spawning the Claude subprocess and captured its outputs. Every\n    primitive ``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` / ``Expect.exit_code``\n    receives this exact record so manifest authors get a stable shape\n    regardless of which runner internal path produced the eval.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-010 verbatim (see\n    ``_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS``) so structural traversal, JSON dumping by\n    downstream tools, and review diffs stay deterministic.\n\n    * ``eval_spec``       — parsed manifest row (DM-002 / T01.03). Stored\n      by-reference; ``EvalSpec`` is itself frozen so the runner cannot\n      mutate the spec after the context is built. ExpectCallables read\n      ``eval_spec.id`` / ``eval_spec.expects`` / ``eval_spec.timeout_sec``\n      directly off the context without having to re-thread the spec.\n    * ``home``            — the per-eval ``HomeIsolation`` record\n      (DM-006 / T02.04). Carries ``eval_id``, ``home_root``,\n      ``session_id``, ``time_offset_sec`` and the COMP-006 method\n      surface; ExpectCallables that need to call ``home.env()`` /\n      ``home.state_path(...)`` (e.g. ``Expect.settings_json`` reading\n      ``~/.claude/settings.json``) can do so without re-deriving the\n      object.\n    * ``home_path``       — the per-eval ``HOME`` directory created by\n      :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. Stored as a separate ``Path`` so\n      ExpectCallables that only need the path avoid the\n      ``home.home_path`` property indirection (and the ``RuntimeError``\n      it raises when setup has not run — by the time the context is\n      built the setup has already completed). The factory keeps\n      ``home.home_path == home_path`` invariant.\n    * ``artifacts_dir``   — directory the runner writes per-eval\n      artifacts under (PTY transcripts, hook outputs, captured\n      side-cars). Resolved beneath ``home_path``; primitives that emit\n      diff side-cars (``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` failures) drop\n      them here.\n    * ``run_dir``         — the run-scoped scratch directory the\n      orchestrator created (FR-G2 / T03.16). One ``run_dir`` per\n      ``superclaude eval run`` invocation; per-eval ``HOME``s and\n      artifact directories live beneath it.\n    * ``env``              — frozen mapping of environment variables that\n      were exported to the Claude subprocess (the union of\n      ``HomeIsolation.env()`` plus ``IsolationLayers.env_vars`` plus any\n      caller overrides). Stored as a ``MappingProxyType`` so callers\n      cannot mutate the record post-build; the factory wraps the supplied\n      mapping in ``MappingProxyType`` to enforce this.\n    * ``stdout_path``      — filesystem path of the captured stdout\n      transcript (the PTY-stripped variant the ``PtyStream`` / T02.17\n      wrote). ExpectCallables that need raw bytes (``Expect.stdout``)\n      open this path.\n    * ``stderr_path``      — same for stderr.\n    * ``transcript_path``  — path to the raw PTY transcript (pre-strip,\n      ANSI escapes preserved). Used by tooling that needs the byte-for-\n      byte capture; ExpectCallables generally use ``stdout_path`` /\n      ``stderr_path`` instead.\n    * ``jsonl_paths``      — frozen mapping of named JSONL log file\n      paths (e.g. ``{\"hook_log\": ..., \"telemetry\": ...}``). Per-eval\n      runtime logs land here; ``Expect.jsonl(name=\"hook_log\", ...)``\n      resolves the path through this mapping. Stored as a\n      ``MappingProxyType`` for the same immutability reason as ``env``.\n    * ``exit_code``        — captured exit code of the Claude subprocess\n      (``int``). The runner records the value the PTY driver observed\n      after ``wait()``; ``Expect.exit_code`` reads it.\n    * ``stdout``           — full captured stdout as a ``str``. Mirrors\n      what ``stdout_path`` points at so ExpectCallables can read it\n      in-memory without re-opening the file. Empty when no output was\n      captured.\n    * ``stderr``           — full captured stderr; same rationale as\n      ``stdout``.\n    * ``duration_sec``     — wall-clock seconds the eval body ran\n      (subprocess spawn to exit). ``float`` for sub-millisecond\n      resolution. ``Expect.duration`` reads this verbatim.\n    * ``artifacts``        — frozen mapping of artifact-name to absolute\n      (or run-relative) path produced by the eval. Mirrors\n      ``EvalOutcome.artifacts`` (DM-001) — the post-run inventory the\n      runner builds before emitting the outcome. Stored as a\n      ``MappingProxyType`` so the context cannot be tampered with by\n      ExpectCallables that share the record across primitives.\n\n    The dataclass is ``frozen=True`` so any attempted ``ctx.foo = bar``\n    raises :class:`dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError` — the contract every\n    ExpectCallable can rely on. Mapping fields are additionally wrapped\n    in :class:`types.MappingProxyType` to prevent the more subtle attack\n    of mutating ``ctx.env[\"HOME\"]`` (frozen-dataclass blocks\n    ``__setattr__``, not method calls on field values).\n\n    Use :meth:`from_runner_state` to construct an ``EvalContext`` from\n    the runner's internal state; direct ``EvalContext(...)`` construction\n    is permitted (tests build minimal contexts for ExpectCallable unit\n    tests) but the factory is the single place runner-internal mappings\n    are wrapped in ``MappingProxyType`` and the ``home.home_path ==\n    home_path`` invariant is enforced.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_spec: \"EvalSpec\"\n    home: \"HomeIsolation\"\n    home_path: Path\n    artifacts_dir: Path\n    run_dir: Path\n    env: Mapping[str, str]\n    stdout_path: Path\n    stderr_path: Path\n    transcript_path: Path\n    jsonl_paths: Mapping[str, Path]\n    exit_code: int\n    stdout: str\n    stderr: str\n    duration_sec: float\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str]\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # Wrap mapping fields in MappingProxyType so callers that share\n        # the context across ExpectCallables cannot mutate the underlying\n        # mapping. Frozen-dataclass only blocks attribute assignment; the\n        # mapping objects themselves stay mutable unless we proxy them.\n        # ``object.__setattr__`` is the documented escape hatch.\n        for name in (\"env\", \"jsonl_paths\", \"artifacts\"):\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if not isinstance(value, MappingProxyType):\n                object.__setattr__(self, name, MappingProxyType(dict(value)))\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_runner_state(\n        cls,\n        *,\n        eval_spec: \"EvalSpec\",\n        home: \"HomeIsolation\",\n        run_dir: Path,\n        artifacts_dir: Path,\n        stdout_path: Path,\n        stderr_path: Path,\n        transcript_path: Path,\n        jsonl_paths: Mapping[str, Path],\n        env: Mapping[str, str],\n        exit_code: int,\n        stdout: str,\n        stderr: str,\n        duration_sec: float,\n        artifacts: Mapping[str, str],\n    ) -> \"EvalContext\":\n        \"\"\"Build an ``EvalContext`` from EvalRunner internal state.\n\n        Centralises three concerns the runner would otherwise have to\n        repeat for every emitted context:\n\n        1. Resolves ``home_path`` off the ``HomeIsolation`` instance so\n           the runner does not duplicate ``home.home_path`` at the call\n           site. The property accessor raises\n           :class:`RuntimeError` if :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` has not\n           run — which is exactly the failure we want surfaced at\n           context construction (rather than later when an\n           ExpectCallable reads ``ctx.home_path``).\n        2. Wraps the three mapping arguments (``env``, ``jsonl_paths``,\n           ``artifacts``) in :class:`types.MappingProxyType` so the\n           context's mapping fields are read-only views of the runner's\n           internal state. ``__post_init__`` would also wrap them, but\n           doing it here keeps the wrap close to the call site for\n           readability.\n        3. Produces a deterministic instance: every ``EvalContext``\n           built from the same arguments compares equal (the\n           ``@dataclass``-generated ``__eq__`` covers the 15 fields; the\n           mapping proxies compare equal when their underlying dicts do).\n\n        Keyword-only arguments are required so future field additions\n        (e.g. when DM-010 grows a 16th field) do not silently re-order\n        positional callers.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return cls(\n            eval_spec=eval_spec,\n            home=home,\n            home_path=home.home_path,\n            artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n            run_dir=run_dir,\n            env=MappingProxyType(dict(env)),\n            stdout_path=stdout_path,\n            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n            jsonl_paths=MappingProxyType(dict(jsonl_paths)),\n            exit_code=exit_code,\n            stdout=stdout,\n            stderr=stderr,\n            duration_sec=duration_sec,\n            artifacts=MappingProxyType(dict(artifacts)),\n        )\n\n\n# Field order mirrors the DM-012 ``counts`` sub-object verbatim so the\n# Reporter's JSON output and any schema validator iterates in the same\n# canonical order across runs.\n_RUN_COUNTS_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"manifest_n\",\n    \"expanded_n_prime\",\n    \"kept_k\",\n    \"skipped_s\",\n    \"kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass RunCounts:\n    \"\"\"Counts sub-structure for ``RunSummary`` (DM-004 / DM-012).\n\n    Five-field Used as the ground-truth row count by\n      the FR-RPT1 invariant guard.\n    * ``kept_k``                           — number of expanded rows that\n      ran end-to-end (status in ``{PASS,FAIL,ERRORED,TIMEOUT,XFAIL,XPASS}``).\n    * ``skipped_s``                        — number of expanded rows that\n      were skipped (status ``SKIPPED`` or ``INTERRUPTED``).\n    * ``kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime`` — boolean assertion documented\n      in DM-012 that the equation ``kept_k + skipped_s == expanded_n_prime``\n      holds. ``RunSummary.__post_init__`` raises ``ValueError`` when the\n      flag and the actual math disagree, so a misreporting orchestrator\n      fails loudly instead of emitting an internally inconsistent summary.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    manifest_n: int\n    expanded_n_prime: int\n    kept_k: int\n    skipped_s: int\n    kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime: bool\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-012 counts sub-object.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_RUN_COUNTS_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _RUN_COUNTS_FIELDS}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors the DM-012 ``totals`` sub-object verbatim.\n_RUN_TOTALS_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"passed\",\n    \"failed\",\n    \"skipped\",\n    \"errored\",\n    \"interrupted\",\n    \"timeout\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass RunTotals:\n    \"\"\"Per-status tally sub-structure for ``RunSummary`` (DM-004 / DM-012).\n\n    Six-field outcome tally that the Reporter surfaces in the headline of\n    ``summary.md`` / ``summary.json``. Carries the run-wide metadata, the per-status tally\n    (``totals``), the row accounting (``counts``), and the per-eval rows\n    (``evals``) the Reporter renders.\n\n    * ``run_id``           — opaque identifier the orchestrator stamped on\n      the run. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips\n      through JSON without bespoke datetime serialisation.\n    * ``finished_at``      — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the\n      orchestrator stop (after the last eval emitted its outcome). May be\n      empty for partial summaries written on SIGINT before the\n      orchestrator completed.\n    * ``duration_sec``     — wall-clock seconds the orchestrator ran end\n      to end. ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``suite``            — manifest path / suite identifier the\n      orchestrator loaded. Helps the Reporter detect mismatched evals when a\n      manifest is bumped between runs.\n    * ``parallel``         — concurrency level the orchestrator scheduled\n      at (post-clamp to ``[1,15]``). The Reporter surfaces this so\n      reviewers can correlate the wall-clock budget against the scheduler\n      knob.\n    * ``counts``           — ``RunCounts`` instance (DM-012 counts sub-\n      structure). ``__post_init__`` asserts the\n      ``kept_k + skipped_s == expanded_n_prime`` equation matches the\n      ``kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime`` flag; mismatched counts raise\n      ``ValueError`` so a misreporting orchestrator fails loudly.\n    * ``totals``           — ``RunTotals`` instance (DM-012 totals sub-\n      structure). FR-RPT1 (T03.11)\n      enforces ``len(evals) == counts.expanded_n_prime`` *outside* this\n      model so partial summaries (SIGINT) can still be constructed for\n      writing.\n    * ``artifacts``        — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n      run-relative) path produced by the orchestrator (e.g. the JSONL\n      directory, the disk-budget breach side-car). Stored as an immutable\n      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n      ``to_dict()`` returns a shallow copy so callers that mutate the\n      returned mapping do not affect the immutable source.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-004 verbatim (see\n    ``_RUN_SUMMARY_FIELDS``) so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n    Reporter snapshots and review diffs.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    run_id: str\n    started_at: str\n    finished_at: str\n    duration_sec: float\n    suite: str\n    manifest_version: str\n    parallel: int\n    counts: RunCounts\n    totals: RunTotals\n    evals: tuple[EvalOutcome, ...] = ()\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # DM-012 declares ``kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime`` as the\n        # boolean assertion that the equation holds. We enforce that the\n        # flag and the actual math agree at construction time so a\n        # misreporting orchestrator cannot emit an internally inconsistent\n        # summary: the FR-RPT1 invariant guard relies on\n        # ``counts.expanded_n_prime`` being authoritative.\n        actual = (\n            self.counts.kept_k + self.counts.skipped_s == self.counts.expanded_n_prime\n        )\n        if self.counts.kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime != actual:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"RunSummary.counts.kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime claims \"\n                f\"{self.counts.kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime!r} but \"\n                f\"kept_k({self.counts.kept_k}) + skipped_s({self.counts.skipped_s}) \"\n                f\"{'==' if actual else '!='} expanded_n_prime({self.counts.expanded_n_prime})\"\n            )\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-004.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_RUN_SUMMARY_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. Nested ``RunCounts`` / ``RunTotals`` / ``EvalOutcome``\n        values are unwrapped via their own ``to_dict()`` so the Reporter\n        never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-copied\n        into a plain ``dict`` so callers that mutate the returned mapping\n        do not affect the immutable source.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _RUN_SUMMARY_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name in (\"counts\", \"totals\"):\n                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n            elif name == \"evals\":\n                payload[name] = [item.to_dict() for item in value]\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n","numLines":938,"startLine":1,"totalLines":938}}}

Run-id determinism\n46\t\n47\t`compose_run_id(started_at, suite_name)` returns the same string for the\n48\tsame `(started_at, suite_name)` pair — the FR-G4 acceptance criterion\n49\tthe phase-4 tasklist pins (\"Run-id is deterministic for a given start\n50\ttimestamp + suite name\").\n51\t\n52\t* **Shape:** `<HHMMSSZ>-<8-hex>`\n53\t* **Time prefix:** `HHMMSSZ` is the UTC time-of-day of `started_at`.\n54\t  Pure-numeric so the id sorts lexicographically by wall-clock time\n55\t  within a date bucket.\n56\t* **Hash tail:** First 8 hex chars of\n57\t  `sha256(suite_name + \"\\n\" + started_at)`. Folding `suite_name` in\n58\t  guarantees runs against different suites at the same instant land in\n59\t  distinct directories.\n60\t* **Collision risk:** 8 hex chars = 32 bits. The hash is keyed by an\n61\t  ISO-second timestamp, so collisions require two runs *of distinct\n62\t  suites* at the exact same wall-clock second. The CI fleet does not\n63\t  approach this density.\n64\t\n65\t### Why not a random suffix?\n66\t\n67\tA random suffix would prevent reproducibility — the AC explicitly\n68\trequires determinism so an operator can re-derive the run-id from\n69\t`(started_at, suite_name)` alone (e.g. to look up a historical run\n70\twithout consulting the run's own manifest).\n71\t\n72\t## 4. Mkdir is the\n76\tcaller's responsibility, and **must happen after**\n77\t`resolve_scratch_root` (the AC12 / T01.19 enforcement boundary)\n78\tvalidates the constructed path against the allowlist.\n79\t\n80\t`allocate_per_eval_paths(run_dir, eval_id, create=True)` is the only\n81\thelper that creates directories — it mkdir's the per-eval directory and\n82\tthe `artifacts/` subdirectory. The `logs.jsonl` and `tty.transcript`\n83\tfiles are not pre-created; the runner / PtyDriver open them lazily on\n84\tfirst write so an unused eval leaves a clean subtree.\n85\t\n86\t## 5. Routing\n87\t\n88\t* **Reporter writes** (`summary.md` / `summary.json` / `summary.yaml` /\n89\t  `junit.xml`) — routed via `Reporter(summary, emit_junit).write(run_dir)`\n90\t  in `commands.eval_run`. The run_dir is the path\n91\t  `compose_run_dir(...)` constructs when `--output-dir` is unset, or the\n92\t  operator-supplied directory otherwise (in which case the AC12\n93\t  allowlist still gates the resolution).\n94\t* **Per-eval logs** (`logs.jsonl`) — routed via the per-eval worker\n95\t  closure that T04.10 wires up. The worker calls\n96\t  `allocate_per_eval_paths(run_dir, eval_id)` and threads the resulting\n97\t  paths into the `EvalRunner` constructor (`log_dir` override) and the\n98\t  PtyDriver (`transcript_path`).\n99\t* **Eval artifacts** (`per-eval/<eval_id>/artifacts/`) — populated by\n100\t  whatever ExpectCallables or executors choose to drop files there.\n101\t  The directory is pre-created so callers do not need to repeat the\n102\t  `mkdir(parents=True)` boilerplate.\n103\t\n104\t## 6. AC12 interaction\n105\t\n106\t`artifact_layout` constructs paths under `.dev/eval-runs/` so a default\n107\trun lands inside the canonical AC12 prefix\n108\t(`_default_allowed_scratch_roots` in `config.py`). The CLI body still\n109\tcalls `resolve_scratch_root(requested_output, config=base_config,\n110\toutput_dir=output_dir)` before the first `mkdir`; that call is the\n111\tsingle enforcement boundary. This module does not duplicate the check.\n112\t\n113\tIf the operator passes `--output-dir <X>`, `compose_run_dir` is **not\n114\tcalled** — the CLI uses `X` directly. AC12 then validates `X` exactly\n115\tas it did before T04.13.\n116\t\n117\t## 7. Backwards compatibility\n118\t\n119\t* `--output-dir <X>` continues to write the four summary files directly\n120\t  under `X` (the T04.11 FR-G6 smoke contract).\n121\t* The default behaviour (no `--output-dir`) gains the nested\n122\t  `.dev/eval-runs/<date>/<run-id>/` structure that T04.13 introduces.\n123\t  This is a no-op for callers that already pass `--output-dir`.\n124\t* `EvalRunner` retains its existing\n125\t  `home_path/.eval-logs/<eval_id>.jsonl` log destination; T04.10 will\n126\t  add a `log_dir` constructor parameter so the per-run worker can\n127\t  re-route logs into the FR-G4 per-eval subtree without breaking the\n128\t  default-home path that existing tests rely on.\n129\t\n130\t## 8. Goal\n\nPin the on-disk shape of every `superclaude eval run` invocation so a\nreviewer (or CI consumer) can locate the artifacts produced by any past\nrun by date + run-id alone — without grep-and-prayer over the\nfilesystem. The layout is the contract every downstream tool (the\nReporter, the per-eval JSONL emitter, the JUnit converter) writes\nagainst.\n\n## 2. Layout\n\n```\n<output_root>/.dev/eval-runs/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<run-id>/\n    summary.md\n    summary.json\n    summary.yaml\n    junit.xml          (only when --junit is set)\n    per-eval/\n        <eval_id>/\n            logs.jsonl\n            tty.transcript\n            artifacts/\n```\n\n| Segment | Source | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|\n| `<output_root>` | `Path.cwd()` by default; `--output-dir` overrides | Anchor under which `.dev/eval-runs/` lives. Default lands inside the canonical AC12 (T01.19 / D-0016) scratch-root allowlist. |\n| `.dev/eval-runs/` | `artifact_layout.RUN_DIR_PREFIX` | Repo-relative prefix. Matches `EvalConfig._default_allowed_scratch_roots`. |\n| `<YYYY-MM-DD>` | UTC ISO date of `started_at` | Per-day bucket so reviewers can navigate by date without exploding `ls`. |\n| `<run-id>` | `compose_run_id(started_at, suite_name)` | `<HHMMSSZ>-<8-hex>` — see §3. |\n| `summary.{md,json,yaml}` | `Reporter.write()` (COMP-008 / D-0055) | DM-004 aggregated run summary in three formats. |\n| `junit.xml` | `Reporter(emit_junit=True).write()` | Optional; FR-RPT2 feature-gated. |\n| `per-eval/<eval_id>/` | `allocate_per_eval_paths(run_dir, eval_id)` | Self-contained subtree for one eval. |\n| `logs.jsonl` | `EvalRunner` JSONL emitter (T03.05 / D-0049) | Per-eval lifecycle event log. |\n| `tty.transcript` | `PtyDriver` (T02.10 / D-0033) | PTY capture for failure forensics. |\n| `artifacts/` | Per-eval ExpectCallables / executor | Free-form artifact drop for the eval (jsonl logs, screenshots, etc.). |\n\n## 3. Run-id determinism\n\n`compose_run_id(started_at, suite_name)` returns the same string for the\nsame `(started_at, suite_name)` pair — the FR-G4 acceptance criterion\nthe phase-4 tasklist pins (\"Run-id is deterministic for a given start\ntimestamp + suite name\").\n\n* **Shape:** `<HHMMSSZ>-<8-hex>`\n* **Time prefix:** `HHMMSSZ` is the UTC time-of-day of `started_at`.\n  Pure-numeric so the id sorts lexicographically by wall-clock time\n  within a date bucket.\n* **Hash tail:** First 8 hex chars of\n  `sha256(suite_name + \"\\n\" + started_at)`. Folding `suite_name` in\n  guarantees runs against different suites at the same instant land in\n  distinct directories.\n* **Collision risk:** 8 hex chars = 32 bits. The hash is keyed by an\n  ISO-second timestamp, so collisions require two runs *of distinct\n  suites* at the exact same wall-clock second. The CI fleet does not\n  approach this density.\n\n### Why not a random suffix?\n\nA random suffix would prevent reproducibility — the AC explicitly\nrequires determinism so an operator can re-derive the run-id from\n`(started_at, suite_name)` alone (e.g. to look up a historical run\nwithout consulting the run's own manifest).\n\n## 4. Construction vs. creation\n\n`compose_run_dir` and `compose_per_eval_dir` are **path-construction**\nhelpers — they return paths but do not touch disk. Mkdir is the\ncaller's responsibility, and **must happen after**\n`resolve_scratch_root` (the AC12 / T01.19 enforcement boundary)\nvalidates the constructed path against the allowlist.\n\n`allocate_per_eval_paths(run_dir, eval_id, create=True)` is the only\nhelper that creates directories — it mkdir's the per-eval directory and\nthe `artifacts/` subdirectory. The `logs.jsonl` and `tty.transcript`\nfiles are not pre-created; the runner / PtyDriver open them lazily on\nfirst write so an unused eval leaves a clean subtree.\n\n## 5. Routing\n\n* **Reporter writes** (`summary.md` / `summary.json` / `summary.yaml` /\n  `junit.xml`) — routed via `Reporter(summary, emit_junit).write(run_dir)`\n  in `commands.eval_run`. The run_dir is the path\n  `compose_run_dir(...)` constructs when `--output-dir` is unset, or the\n  operator-supplied directory otherwise (in which case the AC12\n  allowlist still gates the resolution).\n* **Per-eval logs** (`logs.jsonl`) — routed via the per-eval worker\n  closure that T04.10 wires up. The worker calls\n  `allocate_per_eval_paths(run_dir, eval_id)` and threads the resulting\n  paths into the `EvalRunner` constructor (`log_dir` override) and the\n  PtyDriver (`transcript_path`).\n* **Eval artifacts** (`per-eval/<eval_id>/artifacts/`) — populated by\n  whatever ExpectCallables or executors choose to drop files there.\n  The directory is pre-created so callers do not need to repeat the\n  `mkdir(parents=True)` boilerplate.\n\n## 6. AC12 interaction\n\n`artifact_layout` constructs paths under `.dev/eval-runs/` so a default\nrun lands inside the canonical AC12 prefix\n(`_default_allowed_scratch_roots` in `config.py`). The CLI body still\ncalls `resolve_scratch_root(requested_output, config=base_config,\noutput_dir=output_dir)` before the first `mkdir`; that call is the\nsingle enforcement boundary. Four FR-RPT1 +\nDM-012 obligations have to hold for any caller that turns a\n:class:`RunSummary` into bytes:\n\n1. **N'-vs-K equality** — ``len(summary.evals) ==\n   counts.expanded_n_prime``. A summary that satisfies the dimensional\n   invariant must render through every emitter (``to_markdown``,\n   ``to_json``, ``to_yaml``, ``to_junit``) without raising.\n\n2. **SKIPPED inclusion** — design-spec §9 declares that SKIPPED rows\n   stay in ``evals[]`` (with ``skip_reason`` populated) so the row\n   accounting never tries to \"compress\" the array. the *mismatch* fixture: the\n    Reporter sees ``len(evals) != expanded_n_prime`` and raises.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    auto_skipped = sum(1 for o in evals if o.status in {\"SKIPPED\", \"INTERRUPTED\"})\n Four FR-RPT1 +\nDM-012 obligations have to hold for any caller that turns a\n:class:`RunSummary` into bytes:\n\n1. **N'-vs-K equality** — ``len(summary.evals) ==\n   counts.expanded_n_prime``. A summary that satisfies the dimensional\n   invariant must render through every emitter (``to_markdown``,\n   ``to_json``, ``to_yaml``, ``to_junit``) without raising.\n\n2. **SKIPPED inclusion** — design-spec §9 declares that SKIPPED rows\n   stay in ``evals[]`` (with ``skip_reason`` populated) so the row\n   accounting never tries to \"compress\" the array. The JSON the\n   Reporter writes must surface those rows verbatim — both at the row\n   level (``evals[].status == \"SKIPPED\"`` with ``skip_reason``) and at\n   the count level (``counts.skipped_s`` reflects the tally).\n\n3. **Mismatch failure → exit code 2** — when the invariant is\n   violated, every emitter raises :class:`ReporterContractViolation`\n   *before* writing any artefact, and the violation maps to process\n   exit code 2 (``REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE``). Design-spec\n   §4 classifies a Reporter contract violation as a harness contract\n   error: the orchestrator's accounting and the rendered ``evals[]``\n   disagree, so the run itself is suspect and no partial artefact is\n   allowed on disk.\n\n4. **JSON schema fidelity** — the JSON payload the Reporter renders\n   validates against ``summary.schema.json`` (DM-012 / T03.10). This\n   is the cross-link FR-RPT1 ↔ DM-012: the schema documents the shape\n   the Reporter contracts to write.\n\nThese four scenarios anchor TEST-007 in the phase-4 tasklist\n(``§T04.17``). The test module is deliberately self-contained — it\nbuilds RunSummary fixtures from scratch rather than reusing\n``test_run_report.py`` helpers — so a future refactor of the writer\nhelpers cannot _pass(eval_id: str) -> EvalOutcome:\n    return EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=eval_id,\n        title=f\"{eval_id} pass\",\n        status=\"PASS\",\n        duration_sec=1.5,\n        expects=(ExpectResult(name=\"exit_code\", passed=True, message=\"ok\"),),\n        artifacts={\"stdout\": f\"evals/{eval_id}/stdout.log\"},\n    )\n\n\ndef _skipped(eval_id: str, reason: str = \"capability_gate:mcp_server.tavily\") -> EvalOutcome:\n    return EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=eval_id,\n        title=f\"{eval_id} skipped\",\n        status=\"SKIPPED\",\n        duration_sec=0.0,\n        skip_reason=reason,\n        skip_flag_triggered=\"--no-mcp\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _fail(eval_id: str) -> EvalOutcome:\n    return EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=eval_id,\n        title=f\"{eval_id} fail\",\n        status=\"FAIL\",\n        duration_sec=3.25,\n        expects=(\n            ExpectResult(name=\"exit_code\", passed=False, message=\"expected 0 got 1\"),\n        ),\n    )\n\n\ndef _summary(\n    *,\n    evals: tuple[EvalOutcome, ...],\n    expanded_n_prime: int | None = None,\n    kept_k: int | None = None,\n    skipped_s: int | None = None,\n) -> RunSummary:\n    \"\"\"Build a RunSummary with auto-derived counts unless overridden.\n\n    Overriding ``expanded_n_prime`` to a value other than ``len(evals)``\n    is the canonical way to construct the *mismatch* fixture: the\n    Reporter sees ``len(evals) != expanded_n_prime`` and raises.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    auto_skipped = sum(1 for o in evals if o.status in {\"SKIPPED\", \"INTERRUPTED\"})\n    auto_kept = len(evals) - auto_skipped\n    expanded = expanded_n_prime if expanded_n_prime is not None else len(evals)\n    kept = kept_k if kept_k is not None else auto_kept\n    skipped = skipped_s if skipped_s is not None else auto_skipped\n\n    counts = RunCounts(\n        manifest_n=expanded,\n        expanded_n_prime=expanded,\n        kept_k=kept,\n        skipped_s=skipped,\n        kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime=(kept + skipped == expanded),\n    )\n\n    totals = RunTotals(\n        passed=sum(1 for o in evals if o.status == \"PASS\"),\n        failed=sum(1 for o in evals if o.status == \"FAIL\"),\n        skipped=sum(1 for o in evals if o.status == \"SKIPPED\"),\n        errored=sum(1 for o in evals if o.status == \"ERRORED\"),\n        interrupted=sum(1 for o in evals if o.status == \"INTERRUPTED\"),\n        timeout=sum(1 for o in evals if o.status == \"TIMEOUT\"),\n    )\n\n    return RunSummary(\n        run_id=\"run-test007\",\n        started_at=\"2026-05-20T12:00:00Z\",\n        finished_at=\"2026-05-20T12:00:30Z\",\n        duration_sec=30.0,\n        suite=\"tests/fixtures/cliEval/real.yaml\",\n        manifest_version=\"1.0\",\n        parallel=4,\n        counts=counts,\n        totals=totals,\n        evals=evals,\n    )\n\n\n@pytest.fixture(scope=\"module\")\ndef schema_validator() -> The violation is the design-spec §4 exit-code-2 path.\n    assert REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE == 2\n    # And the exception type itself is the one a CLI dispatcher catches.\n    assert isinstance(writer_exc.value, ReporterContractViolation)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Scenario 4 — JSON schema fidelity\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_reporter_json_validates_against_summary_schema(\n    tmp_path: Path, schema_validator: Draft202012Validator\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"FR-RPT1 ↔ DM-012 scenario 4: the JSON payload the Reporter renders\n    validates against ``summary.schema.json``.\n\n    Pins the cross-link between FR-RPT1 (the writer) and DM-012 (the\n    contract). A summary covering every distinct row class — PASS,\n    SKIPPED, FAIL, ERRORED — must validate cleanly. The check runs\n    twice: once on the in-memory string from\n    :meth:`Reporter.to_json` and once on the bytes the writer drops to\n    disk via :meth:`Reporter.write`. Both must be schema-valid so a\n    refactor cannot silently diverge the two surfaces.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    errored = EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=\"E5\",\n        title=\"errored eval\",\n        status=\"ERRORED\",\n        duration_sec=2.0,\n        error_class=\"builtins.RuntimeError\",\n    )\n    summary = _summary(evals=(_pass(\"E1\"), _skipped(\"E2\"), _fail(\"E3\"), errored))\n\n    # 1) The in-memory JSON string validates.\n    reporter = Reporter(summary)\n    in_memory_payload = json.loads(reporter.to_json())\n    schema_validator.validate(in_memory_payload)\n\n    # 2) The on-disk JSON file validates (and matches the in-memory copy).\n    written = reporter.write(tmp_path)\n    on_disk_payload = json.loads(written[\"summary.json\"].read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    schema_validator.validate(on_disk_payload)\n    assert on_disk_payload == in_memory_payload, (\n        \"Reporter.to_json() and the file write must produce identical payloads\"\n    )\n\n    # The payload covers every distinct DM-012 row shape (PASS, SKIPPED,\n    # FAIL, ERRORED) so the schema fidelity check exercises every\n    # ``oneOf`` branch the schema declares.\n    statuses = {row[\"status\"] for row in on_disk_payload[\"evals\"]}\n    assert statuses == {\"PASS\", \"SKIPPED\", \"FAIL\", \"ERRORED\"}\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}

Goal\n\nPin the four load-bearing TEST-007 obligations the Reporter (COMP-008)\nmust satisfy for downstream consumers (CI summary jobs, the JUnit\npipeline, the `superclaude eval run` exit-code dispatcher). The Reporter\nis the single point where the orchestrator's accounting becomes\nmachine-readable bytes; if its contract drifts, every downstream consumer\nsilently inherits the drift. These tests stand as the canonical\nregression line.\n\n## 2. Test matrix\n\nThe four pytest cases in `tests/cli/eval/test_reporter_contract.py`\ncorrespond one-to-one with the TEST-007 scenarios named in the phase-4\ntasklist:\n\n| # | Scenario | Test function | Pins |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | **N'-vs-K equality** | `test_n_prime_equals_k_lets_every_emitter_render` | When `len(summary.evals) == counts.expanded_n_prime`, every emitter (`to_markdown`, `to_json`, `to_yaml`, `to_junit`) renders without raising; `Reporter.write(...)` drops the canonical four-file artefact set; JUnit `<testsuite tests=\"...\">` mirrors `expanded_n_prime`. |\n| 2 | **SKIPPED inclusion** | `test_skipped_rows_included_in_evals_with_skip_reason` | SKIPPED rows survive in `summary.json :: evals[]` with `status=\"SKIPPED\"`, `skip_reason` and `skip_flag_triggered` populated; per-status `totals.skipped` and `counts.skipped_s` reflect the tally; the markdown table surfaces the same rows + reasons. |\n| 3 | **Mismatch → exit code 2** | `test_n_prime_vs_k_mismatch_raises_and_maps_to_exit_code_two` | A mismatched summary (`len(evals) != counts.expanded_n_prime`) raises `ReporterContractViolation` from every emitter and from `write_aggregated_report` *before* any artefact is written to disk; `REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE == 2`; the exception carries `expected`, `actual`, and `run_id` plus a diagnostic message including both sides of the mismatch. |\n| 4 | **JSON schema fidelity** | `test_reporter_json_validates_against_summary_schema` | The JSON the Reporter renders validates against `summary.schema.json` (DM-012 / T03.10) on *both* the in-memory string from `Reporter.to_json()` and the bytes the writer drops on disk via `Reporter.write(...)`; both payloads compare equal so a refactor cannot divergence them. The fixture covers every distinct row class (PASS / SKIPPED / FAIL / ERRORED) so every `oneOf` branch in the schema is exercised. |\n\n## 3. Fixture design\n\n`_summary(evals=..., expanded_n_prime=..., kept_k=..., skipped_s=...)`\nauto-derives the counts when overrides are not supplied. Overriding\n`expanded_n_prime` to a value other than `len(evals)` is the canonical\nway to construct the mismatch fixture: the Reporter sees\n`len(evals) != counts.expanded_n_prime` and raises.\n\nFixtures are intentionally self-contained — they do not reuse\n`test_run_report.py` helpers — so a refactor of the writer helpers\ncannot quietly drop the contract guarantees this module pins.\n\n## 4. Exit code 2 mapping (Scenario 3 detail)\n\nDesign-spec §4 maps the four eval-run exit codes as follows:\n\n| Code | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| 0 | All evals satisfied their `expects:` blocks; no FAIL / ERRORED / TIMEOUT / XPASS. |\n| 1 | At least one eval failed; orchestrator finished cleanly. |\n| **2** | **Harness contract error — the orchestrator's accounting and the rendered `evals[]` disagree. Reporter contract violation lives here.** |\n| 3 | Interrupted (SIGINT) before the orchestrator completed. |\n\n`REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` is the integer the CLI\ndispatcher returns when it catches `ReporterContractViolation` — the\nrunner / orchestrator catches the exception at the run boundary and\nexits with this constant. The test pins the constant value (`== 2`) so\na future refactor that changes the constant fails loudly here rather\nthan silently in a downstream consumer.\n\nThe TEST-008 exit-code suite (T04.19) covers the process-level exit\ncode through `subprocess.run` against `superclaude eval run`. Goal\n\nPin the four load-bearing TEST-007 obligations the Reporter (COMP-008)\nmust satisfy for downstream consumers (CI summary jobs, the JUnit\npipeline, the `superclaude eval run` exit-code dispatcher). The Reporter\nis the single point where the orchestrator's accounting becomes\nmachine-readable bytes; if its contract drifts, every downstream consumer\nsilently inherits the drift. These tests stand as the canonical\nregression line.\n\n## 2. Test matrix\n\nThe four pytest cases in `tests/cli/eval/test_reporter_contract.py`\ncorrespond one-to-one with the TEST-007 scenarios named in the phase-4\ntasklist:\n\n| # | Scenario | Test function | Pins |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | **N'-vs-K equality** | `test_n_prime_equals_k_lets_every_emitter_render` | When `len(summary.evals) == counts.expanded_n_prime`, every emitter (`to_markdown`, `to_json`, `to_yaml`, `to_junit`) renders without raising; `Reporter.write(...)` drops the canonical four-file artefact set; JUnit `<testsuite tests=\"...\">` mirrors `expanded_n_prime`. |\n| 2 | **SKIPPED inclusion** | `test_skipped_rows_included_in_evals_with_skip_reason` | SKIPPED rows survive in `summary.json :: evals[]` with `status=\"SKIPPED\"`, `skip_reason` and `skip_flag_triggered` populated; per-status `totals.skipped` and `counts.skipped_s` reflect the tally; the markdown table surfaces the same rows + reasons. |\n| 3 | **Mismatch → exit code 2** | `test_n_prime_vs_k_mismatch_raises_and_maps_to_exit_code_two` | A mismatched summary (`len(evals) != counts.expanded_n_prime`) raises `ReporterContractViolation` from every emitter and from `write_aggregated_report` *before* any artefact is written to disk; `REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE == 2`; the exception carries `expected`, `actual`, and `run_id` plus a diagnostic message including both sides of the mismatch. |\n| 4 | **JSON schema fidelity** | `test_reporter_json_validates_against_summary_schema` | The JSON the Reporter renders validates against `summary.schema.json` (DM-012 / T03.10) on *both* the in-memory string from `Reporter.to_json()` and the bytes the writer drops on disk via `Reporter.write(...)`; both payloads compare equal so a refactor cannot divergence them. The fixture covers every distinct row class (PASS / SKIPPED / FAIL / ERRORED) so every `oneOf` branch in the schema is exercised. |\n\n## 3. Fixture design\n\n`_summary(evals=..., expanded_n_prime=..., kept_k=..., skipped_s=...)`\nauto-derives the counts when overrides are not supplied. Overriding\n`expanded_n_prime` to a value other than `len(evals)` is the canonical\nway to construct the mismatch fixture: the Reporter sees\n`len(evals) != counts.expanded_n_prime` and raises.\n\nFixtures are intentionally self-contained — they do not reuse\n`test_run_report.py` helpers — so a refactor of the writer helpers\ncannot quietly drop the contract guarantees this module pins.\n\n## 4. Exit code 2 mapping (Scenario 3 detail)\n\nDesign-spec §4 maps the four eval-run exit codes as follows:\n\n| Code | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| 0 | All evals satisfied their `expects:` blocks; no FAIL / ERRORED / TIMEOUT / XPASS. |\n| 1 | At least one eval failed; orchestrator finished cleanly. |\n| **2** | **Harness contract error — the orchestrator's accounting and the rendered `evals[]` disagree. Reporter contract violation lives here.** |\n| 3 | Interrupted (SIGINT) before the orchestrator completed. |\n\n`REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` is the integer the CLI\ndispatcher returns when it catches `ReporterContractViolation` — the\nrunner / orchestrator catches the exception at the run boundary and\nexits with this constant. The test pins the constant value (`== 2`) so\na future refactor that changes the constant fails loudly here rather\nthan silently in a downstream consumer.\n\nThe TEST-008 exit-code suite (T04.19) covers the process-level exit\ncode through `subprocess.run` against `superclaude eval run`.
